| [Albania](738.md) | 15 September 1984 | Albania opened an embassy in Canberra in 2020 and has honorary consulates in Adelaide and Brisbane. Australia's embassy in Rome is accredited to Albania Albania's foreign minister [Edmond Panariti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmond_Panariti) visited Australia in August 2012, following on from a visit to Albania from [Richard Marles](4386805.md), parliamentary secretary for foreign affairs.[^cite_note-602] There are approximately 11,000 people in Australia of Albanian descent. In recent years, the Australian Government has provided financial and humanitarian assistance to Albania. |
| [Andorra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andorra) | 2 March 1995 | Australia and Andorra established diplomatic relations in 1995.[^cite_note-exteriors-209] Australia's first non-resident ambassador presented credentials in 1998. Australia's ambassador is accredited from [Madrid](41188263.md). Andorra does not currently have representation to Australia. |
| [Austria](26964606.md) | 1952 | Australia has an embassy in [Vienna](55866.md). Austria has an embassy in Canberra. |
| [Belarus](3457.md) | 9 January 1992[^cite_note-603] | Australia recognised Belarus' independence on 26 December 1991[^cite_note-Australia_recognizes_the_11_republics_but_not_Georgia-434] upon the dissolution of the [Union of Soviet Socialist Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Socialist_Republics) and established diplomatic relations the following year, becoming the third country to do so.[^cite_note-604] Australia is accredited to Belarus from its embassy in Moscow, Russia.[^cite_note-605] Belarus operated a resident embassy in Canberra until budget cuts forced its closure in 2018. It is currently accredited to Australia from its embassy in Jakarta, Indonesia. Bilateral trade in 2020–21 was valued at A\$34.7 million, mostly Australian fertiliser imports. Since then Australia has imposed trade measures and targeted sanctions on Belarus, in response to its support for Russia's invasion on Ukraine.[^cite_note-606] |
| [Belgium](3343.md) | 1947 | See [Australia–Belgium relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Belgium_relations) Both countries have a growing bilateral commercial relationship, going back to [World War One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_One) when they entered after Belgium was invaded by Germany. They share similar approaches to many international issues, including arms control, whaling and Antarctica. Trade and investment relations are very significant. In 2012, total Belgian investment in Australia was valued at A\$6.4 billion, and Australian investment in Belgium totalled \$2 billion. In 2012, Belgium was ranked as Australia's 24th largest merchandise trading partner.[^cite_note-607] Almost 30 [Australia–Belgium bilateral treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Belgium_bilateral_treaties) cover extradition, trade, taxation, and social security. |
| [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md) | 7 January 1993 | Australia recognised Bosnia and Herzegovina in 1992 and established diplomatic relations in 1994. Australia has a consulate in Sarajevo, Bosnia and Herzegovina has an embassy in Canberra. Australia has a continuing interest in efforts to maintain peace and build prosperity in Bosnia and Herzegovina. Since 1993–94, Australia has contributed humanitarian assistance worth over A\$17 million to countries in the Balkans, including to Bosnia and Herzegovina.[^cite_note-608] |
| [Bulgaria](3415.md) | 5 April 1972 | Australia is represented in Bulgaria through its embassy in [Athens](1216.md) ([Greece](12108.md)). Australia has an honorary consulate in [Sofia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sofia).[^cite_note-609] Bulgaria has an embassy in Canberra.[^cite_note-610] |
| [Croatia](5573.md) | 13 February 1992 | See [Australia–Croatia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Croatia_relations) Australia gave recognition of Croatia in January 1992 Australia has an embassy in [Zagreb](55906.md). Croatia have an embassy in Canberra and consulates in Melbourne, Perth and Sydney. Over 118,000 Australians are of Croatian descent, the largest of the former Yugoslav nations. The two nations have signed a few bilateral agreements such as a social security agreement in May 2003 to give greater protection to people who have lived or worked in Australia and/or Croatia. Bilateral trade is worth A\$42 million.[^cite_note-611] |
| [Cyprus](5593.md) | 19 April 1973[^cite_note-612] | See [Australia–Cyprus relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Cyprus_relations) Australia and Cyprus have enjoyed friendly relations as members of the [Commonwealth of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations) since Cyprus' independence in 1960. Australia and Cyprus formalize diplomatic relations in April 1973 with the accreditation of an Australian high commissioner on a non-resident basis. Australia has a [High Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Commission) in [Nicosia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicosia), opened in August 1975.[^cite_note-613] For more detailed information on Australian representation in Cyprus see [List of high commissioners of Australia to Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_commissioners_of_Australia_to_Cyprus). Cyprus has High Commission in Canberra.[^cite_note-614] Australia recognises the Republic of Cyprus as the only legitimate authority on the island, and does not recognise the Turkish Republic of Northern Cyprus (TRNC). However TRNC maintains honorary representatives in Melbourne and Sydney.[^cite_note-615] Australia, enjoying good relations with both Cyprus and Turkey, has played a role over the years to support negotiations for a settlement in accordance with UN resolutions. Former Australian foreign minister [Alexander Downer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Downer) was a UN special envoy from 2008 to 2014.[^cite_note-616] The [United Nations Peacekeeping Force in Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Peacekeeping_Force_in_Cyprus) was Australia's longest peacekeeping mission. Australian peacekeepers, including Australian police personnel, served in Cyprus for 57 years, from 1964 to 2021.[^cite_note-617] Australia hosts the second largest Cypriot diaspora after the UK. Cypriot-born migrants and their families in Australia number over 80,000. Greek-Cypriots retain a numerical dominance within the Cyprus-born community. The largest Greek-Cypriot communities are in Sydney and Melbourne. Melbourne has the most Turkish-Cypriots.[^cite_note-618] In 2020, Australia's two-way goods and services trade with Cyprus was valued at \$265 million, of which \$232 million were imports from Cyprus. Major merchandise imports from Cyprus to Australia included cheese and curd; alcoholic beverages and medical products.[^cite_note-619] Cypriot president [Dimitris Christofias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dimitris_Christofias) made an official visit to Australia in 2011.[^cite_note-620] |
| [Czech Republic](5321.md) | 18 June 1972/1 January 1993 | Australia is represented in the Czech Republic through its embassy in [Warsaw](32908.md) ([Poland](22936.md)) and through an honorary consulate in [Prague](23844.md).[^cite_note-621] The Czech Republic has an embassy in Canberra, a consulate in Sydney and five honorary consulates (in Melbourne, Perth, Adelaide, Hobart and Brisbane).[^cite_note-622][^cite_note-623] There are around 21,000 people of [Czech descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechs) living in Australia. The [Czechoslovak 11th Infantry Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechoslovak_11th_Infantry_Battalion) fought together with Australia during the [Siege of Tobruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Tobruk) A number of [Australia–Czech Republic bilateral treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Czech_Republic_bilateral_treaties) have been agreed between the two countries, covering extradition, trade, nuclear energy and other matters. [Australian Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade about the relation with the Czech Republic](http://www.dfat.gov.au/geo/czech_republic/index.html) |
| [Denmark](76972.md) | 1947 | See [Australia–Denmark relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Denmark_relations) Australia has an embassy in [Copenhagen](5166.md). Denmark has an embassy in Canberra. |
| [Estonia](28222445.md) | 21 November 1991 | See [Australia–Estonia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Estonia_relations) Sydney [Estonian House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonian_House), part of an extensive cultural network of the Estonian community in Australia. The first stage was opened in 1940, the first of its kind built by Estonians outside of Estonia. In 1935 Estonia opened an honorary consulate in Sydney to promote trade and provide services to Estonians living in Australia. At that time about 2000 Estonians were estimated to be living in [New South Wales](21654.md).[^cite_note-624] Except for a brief period in 1974–75,[^cite_note-Edgars_Dunsdorfs_1975-625] Australia did not recognise the 1940 incorporation of Estonia into the [Union of Soviet Socialist Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Socialist_Republics). However, in October 1940, the Estonian government-in-exile directed the closure of the consulate.[^cite_note-626] Australia was among the first countries to recognise Estonia's restoration of independence on 27 August 1991. Both countries established diplomatic relations on 21 November 1991 with the accreditation of the Australian Ambassador in [Stockholm](26741.md) on a non-resident basis.[^cite_note-Estonia_Country_Brief-627] Australia has an embassy in [Tallinn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallinn). It is Australia's first 'Pop-up Embassy': the Ambassador to Estonia operates from an office in Tallinn during April and May. Outside this period, the Ambassador is based in Canberra but visits Tallinn a number of times a year, and maintains relations via phone, video conferencing, social media and websites. Australia also has ah honorary consulate in Tallinn.[^cite_note-628] Estonia is represented in Australia through its embassy in Canberra, established in 2016, and four honorary consulates, in Adelaide, Hobart, Perth and Brisbane.[^cite_note-Estonia_Country_Brief-627] Australia and Estonia have friendly relations, based on shared interests in promoting democracy, peace and free trade, and work closely in international fora on these subjects. Australia is host to one of the largest communities of Estonians abroad. The 2016 census in Australia recorded over 9,500 people who identified as being of Estonian ancestry. People-to-people exchanges are also supported by a reciprocal working holiday visa scheme between the two countries. Two-way merchandise trade between Australia and Estonia is modest, valued at \$160 million in 2018.[^cite_note-Estonia_Country_Brief-627] President [Kersti Kaljulaid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kersti_Kaljulaid) visited Australia in October 2018 to attend the Invictus Games in Sydney. |
| [Finland](10577.md) | 31 May 1949 | See [Australia–Finland relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Finland_relations) Friendly relations between the two countries date back to Finland's independence. A Finnish consular presence was officially established in 1919.[^cite_note-629] Wartime exigencies led to the breaking of consular relations in July 1941 and the subsequent declaration of war by Australia on 8 December 1941 in symbolic support of its then wartime ally, the [Union of Soviet Socialist Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Socialist_Republics). No shots were ever fired between the two countries and peace was restored under the [Paris Peace Treaties, 1947](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Peace_Treaties,_1947), ratified by Australia in 1948.[^cite_note-630] Diplomatic relations were established with the appointment of the first Finnish Chargé d'affaires in July 1949. The Finnish legation opened in Sydney on 2 August 1949. Diplomatic relations were raised to ambassadorial level in 1968. Australia is represented in Finland through its embassy in [Stockholm](26741.md), Sweden, and through an honorary consulate in [Helsinki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki). Finland has an embassy in Canberra, an honorary consulate-general in Melbourne and honorary consulates in Adelaide, Brisbane, Cairns, Darwin, Hobart, Perth and Sydney. Finnish immigration to Australia has come in waves, particularly in the 1920s and 1950s, peaking by 1971. The 2016 Australian census recorded 7,711 Finland-born residents.[^cite_note-631] In 2021, Australia's two-way goods and services trade with Finland was valued at A\$1.7 billion. The stock of Australian investment in Finland was valued at \$4.4 billion in 2021, while Finnish investment in Australia was valued at \$1.0 billion.[^cite_note-632] President [Tarja Halonen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarja_Halonen) visited Australia in 2007 and this was reciprocated by the state visit of Australian governor-general [Sir Peter Cosgrove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_Peter_Cosgrove) in 2016. Prime Minister [Sanna Marin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanna_Marin) became the first Finnish prime minister to visit Australia in December 2022. |
| [France](5843419.md) | 1944 | See [Australia–France relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93France_relations) France and Australia have a close relationship founded on historical contacts, shared values of democracy and human rights, substantial commercial links, and a keen interest in each other's culture. Australia has an embassy in [Paris](22989.md) and consulates-general in [Nouméa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a) and [Papeete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papeete). France has an embassy in Canberra and a consulate-general in Sydney. |
| [Germany](11867.md) | 28 January 1952 with FRG[^cite_note-633] (22 December 1972 – 3 October 1990 with GDR) | See [Australia–Germany relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Germany_relations) Australia has an embassy in [Berlin](3354.md) and a consulate-general in [Frankfurt](10992.md). Germany has an embassy in Canberra. |
| [Greece](12108.md) | 30 March 1953 | See [Australia–Greece relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Greece_relations) Australia and Greece have a close bilateral relationship based on historical ties and the rich contribution of Greek Australians to Australian society.[^cite_note-634] Australia has an embassy in [Athens](1216.md).[^cite_note-635] Greece has an embassy in Canberra, consulates-general in [Adelaide](1148.md), [Melbourne](17306237.md) and [Sydney](27862.md), a consulate in [Perth](24355.md) and honorary consulates in [Darwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darwin,_Northern_Territory) and [Maitland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maitland,_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-636] Australia and Greece were Allies during WWI and WWII and Australian forces took part in the [Battle of Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Greece) and [Battle of Crete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Crete) in 1941. These are commemorated by the ANZAC Memorial Trail in [Lemnos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemnos),[^cite_note-637] and the [Hellenic Memorial, Canberra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenic_Memorial,_Canberra). Formal diplomatic relations were announced in October 1952 with the appointment of the first Greek minister to Australia[^cite_note-638] who presented credentials on 30 March 1953. Diplomatic relations were raised to ambassadorial level in 1964 with the upgrade of the Australian consulate-general in Athens and the Greek legation in Australia to embassies. For a detailed history of Australian representation to Greece see [List of ambassadors of Australia to Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_Australia_to_Greece). The first official visit to Greece by an Australian prime minister was by [Robert Menzies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Menzies) in 1955.[^cite_note-639][^cite_note-640] Since the 1980s there have been regular exchanges at head of state and government level. Presidents [Konstantinos Karamanlis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantinos_Karamanlis), [Christos Sartzetakis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christos_Sartzetakis) and [Konstantinos Stephanopoulos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantinos_Stephanopoulos) have made state visits in 1982, 1988 and 2002.[^cite_note-641] The most recent Australian state visit was by Governor-general [David Hurley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Hurley) in May 2023.[^cite_note-642] There is a large Greek community in Australia, established in the 19th century and expanded in waves particularly after WWI, WWII and the [Greek Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Civil_War). The 2021 Australian census recorded over 100,000 Greek-born residents and 424,750 residents of Greek descent.[^cite_note-643] Greek immigrants and their descendants have made major contributions across a broad range of sectors in Australia – see [Greek Australian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Australian). Bilateral merchandise and services trade in 2021–22 was just over A\$2.2 billion, a significant element being Greek export of tourism and transportation services.[^cite_note-644] |
| [Holy See](13393.md) | 24 March 1973[^cite_note-645] | The Holy See established an Apostolic Delegation in Sydney in 1914. Formal diplomatic relations were established with Australia under the Whitlam Government in March 1973 and an Apostolic Nunciature was established in Canberra. For more detailed information on the Holy See's representation in Australia see [Apostolic Nunciature to Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Nunciature_to_Australia). From 1973 to 2008, Australia maintained a non-resident ambassador, based in another European capital, as well as an office at the Holy See, headed by a [Counsellor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister-counsellor). On 21 July 2008, the Australian Government announced that it would appoint for the first time a resident ambassador to the Holy See. According to the Australian Foreign Ministry, this marked a significant deepening of Australia's relations with the Vatican since it would allow Australia to expand dialogue with the Vatican in areas including human rights, political and religious freedom, inter-faith dialogue, food security, arms control, refugees and anti-people trafficking, and climate change.[^cite_note-646] Mr [Tim Fischer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Fischer), a former deputy prime minister, presented credentials to Pope Benedict XVI on 12 February 2009. Since then Australia has continued to accredit a resident ambassador. For more detailed information on Australia's representation to the Holy See see [List of ambassadors of Australia to the Holy See](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_Australia_to_the_Holy_See). [Pope Paul VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI) was the first pope to visit Australia in 1970.[^cite_note-647] [Pope John Paul II](23805.md) visited every state and territory in 1986 and [Pope Benedict XVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI) visited Sydney in 2008 for that year's [World Youth Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Youth_Day). The Australian prime minister, Mr Rudd, visited the pope, Benedict XVI, and met the Vatican's secretary of state on 9 July 2009. The secretary for relations with states [Dominique Mamberti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dominique_Mamberti) visited Australia in November 2014, the first such visit to Australia by a Vatican foreign minister. |
| [Hungary](13275.md) | 6 April 1972 | Australia is accredited to Hungary via its embassy in Vienna, Austria, and closed its resident embassy in [Budapest](36787.md) in 2013 due to budget constraints.[^cite_note-648] Hungary has an embassy in Canberra, a consulate general in Sydney and four [honorary consulate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorary_consulate) (in Adelaide, Brisbane, Melbourne and Perth).[^cite_note-649] There are 67,000 people of Hungarian ancestry living in Australia, many Hungarian-Australians have been active in the fields of business, academia, politics and the arts such as [Nick Greiner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Greiner), [Frank Lowy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Lowy), [Judy Cassab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judy_Cassab), and [Les Murray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Murray_\(broadcaster\)) In recent years there have been several high-level visits to strengthen relations. [Australian Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade about relations with Hungary](http://www.dfat.gov.au/geo/hungary/index.html) |
| [Iceland](14531.md) | 17 April 1984 | Australia is accredited to Iceland from its embassy in Copenhagen, Denmark. Iceland is accredited to Australia from its embassy in Beijing, China. |
| [Ireland](14560.md) | 1 July 1946 | See [Australia–Ireland relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Ireland_relations) Australia and Ireland share a close historical relationship with strong cultural links between the two nations. Australia has an embassy in [Dublin](8504.md).[^cite_note-650] Ireland has an embassy in Canberra, a consulate general in Sydney and an honorary consulate in Victoria, and both nations are [former British colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire).[^cite_note-651] About 20% of the Australian population have Irish ancestry, which often predates Irish Independence in 1922. [Australia Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade about relations with Ireland](http://www.dfat.gov.au/geo/ireland/index.html) |
| [Italy](14532.md) | 1948 | See [Australia–Italy relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Italy_relations) Australia has an embassy in Rome and a general consulate in [Milan](36511.md).[^cite_note-652] Italy has an embassy in Canberra and consulates-general in Melbourne and Sydney and honorary consulates in Newcastle,[^cite_note-653] Adelaide, Brisbane and Perth.[^cite_note-654][^cite_note-655][^cite_note-656] Italy and Australia have for long years, sustained a muscular and positively friendly relationship in the categories of immigration, political strategism, and [commercial trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade). There are around 850,000 people of Italian descent living in Australia. [Australian Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade about the relation with Italy](http://www.dfat.gov.au/geo/italy/index.html) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20140328162314/http://dfat.gov.au/geo/italy/index.html) 28 March 2014 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine) |
| [Kosovo](17391.md) | 21 May 2008[^cite_note-657] | See [Australia–Kosovo relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Kosovo_relations) Australia recognised the Republic of Kosovo on 19 February 2008.[^cite_note-658] Australia's Ambassador to Kosovo is subordinate to the embassy in [Vienna](55866.md).[^cite_note-659] Kosovo has an embassy in Canberra.[^cite_note-660] |
| [Latvia](17514.md) | 27 August 1991 | See [Australia–Latvia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Latvia_relations) Except for a brief period in 1974–75,[^cite_note-Edgars_Dunsdorfs_1975-625] Australia did not recognise the 1940 incorporation of Latvia into the [Union of Soviet Socialist Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Socialist_Republics). A Latvian honorary consulate which had opened in Melbourne prior to the Soviet incorporation continued operations throughout the period of occupation. Australia recognised the restoration of Latvian independence and announced its intention to establish diplomatic relations on 27 August 1991.[^cite_note-Prime_Minister's_Media_Release-661] The Australian Ambassador in [Copenhagen](5166.md) was subsequently accredited later that year. Currently the Australian Ambassador in Stockholm is accredited to Latvia and Australia has an honorary consul in [Riga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riga). Latvia has established a resident embassy in Canberra in October 2021, having previously accredited its ambassador based in [Tokyo](30057.md). It also has honorary consulates in Adelaide, Brisbane, Melbourne, Perth and Sydney. Australia and Latvia have friendly relations, based on shared interests in promoting democracy, peace and free trade, and work closely in international fora on these subjects. There are also important people-to-people links between the two countries. Between 1947 and 1952, 19,700 Latvian refugees arrived in Australia having fled the Soviet re-occupation. The resident Australian population born in Latvia (2016 census) was 3,756 and Australian residents of Latvian descent numbered 20,514.[^cite_note-latvia-662] Australia's merchandise trade with Latvia in 2018 was \$83.7 million.[^cite_note-latvia-662] |
| [Liechtenstein](17810.md) | 1999 | Australia and Liechtenstein formalized diplomatic relations in 1999 with the first accreditation on a non-resident basis of the Australian Ambassador in [Berlin](3354.md). Liechtenstein is represented in Australia through the Swiss embassy and consulates. Australia and Liechtenstein work closely to fight modern slavery and human trafficking through the 'Liechtenstein Initiative: Finance Against Slavery and Trafficking'. The Initiative is a public-private partnership that focuses on the finance sector's role in ending modern slavery and human trafficking. Australia, Liechtenstein and the Netherlands are lead countries in the Initiative.[^cite_note-663] |
| [Lithuania](17675.md) | 6 November 1991 | See [Australia–Lithuania relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Lithuania_relations) Except for a brief period in 1974–75,[^cite_note-Edgars_Dunsdorfs_1975-625] Australia did not recognise the 1940 incorporation of Lithuania into the [Union of Soviet Socialist Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Socialist_Republics). Australia recognised the restoration of Lithuanian independence and announced its intention to establish diplomatic relations on 27 August 1991.[^cite_note-Prime_Minister's_Media_Release-661] The Australian Ambassador in [Copenhagen](5166.md) was subsequently accredited later that year. Currently the Australian Ambassador in Warsaw is accredited to Lithuania and Australia has an honorary consul in [Vilnius](32597.md). Lithuania established a resident embassy in Canberra in 2020, having previously accredited its ambassador based in [Tokyo](30057.md). It also has honorary consulates in Sydney, Melbourne and Adelaide. Australia and Lithuania have friendly relations, based on shared interests in promoting democracy, peace and free trade, and work closely in international fora on these subjects. President [Gitanas Nauseda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gitanas_Nauseda) made a state visit to Australia in October 2023.[^cite_note-664] There are also important people-to-people links between the two countries. Approximately 10,000 Lithuanians migrated to Australia, mainly between 1947 and 1953, as refugees fleeing the Soviet re-occupation of Lithuania. They and their descendants have integrated into Australian society but retain cultural and family links with Lithuania.[^cite_note-665] The resident Australian population born in Lithuania (2016 census) was 2,607 and Australian residents of Lithuanian descent numbered 16,292. According to unofficial estimates by the Lithuanian community, however, the actual number could be over 50,000 people.[^cite_note-666] In 2019, Australia's two-way goods and services trade with Lithuania was valued at \$145 million.[^cite_note-667] |
| [Luxembourg](17515.md) | 18 September 1970[^cite_note-668] | See [Australia–Luxembourg relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Luxembourg_relations) Australia and Luxembourg formalized diplomatic relations in September 1970 with the first appointment of Australia's ambassador in [Brussels](3708.md) as non-resident ambassador to Luxembourg. The first ambassador presented credentials on 13 October 1970. Since then Australia has remained represented in Luxembourg through its embassy in [Brussels](3708.md). Luxembourg is represented in Australia through the embassy of the Netherlands[^cite_note-669] in Canberra and through honorary consulates in Sydney and Melbourne. Trade and investment figure strongly in the bilateral relationship and ministerial visits between the countries have focused on this. Luxembourg ranks as Australia's seventh largest investor (stocks) at A\$93 billion in 2021, with Foreign Direct Investments (stocks) of \$10 billion in Australia. Two-way goods and services trade in 2020 was valued at A\$185 million.[^cite_note-670] There is a reciprocal working holiday visa scheme between the two countries. |
| [Malta](19137.md) | 21 September 1964 | See [Australia–Malta relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Malta_relations) Australia has a High Commission in [Valletta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valletta).[^cite_note-671] Malta has a High Commission in Canberra, two consulates-general (in Melbourne and Sydney), and four honorary consulates (in Adelaide, [Ascot Vale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ascot_Vale), Melbourne and Perth).[^cite_note-672] [Australia Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade about relations with Malta](http://www.dfat.gov.au/geo/malta/index.html) |
| [Moldova](19260.md) | 1 April 1992 | Australia recognised Moldova's independence on 26 December 1991[^cite_note-Australia_recognizes_the_11_republics_but_not_Georgia-434] upon the dissolution of the [Union of Soviet Socialist Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Socialist_Republics) and established diplomatic relations the following year. Australia is represented in Moldova by its embassy in [Moscow](19004.md).[^cite_note-673] Moldova has not yet established representation to Australia. |
| [Monaco](19261.md) | 3 May 2007 | Consular relations between Australia and Monaco date back to August 1959 when Monaco's first honorary consul in Australia was appointed. Diplomatic relations between Australia and Monaco were formalized in May 2007 when Australia's first non-resident ambassador presented credentials. Monaco appointed its first non-resident ambassador in September 2007.[^cite_note-674] Australia's ambassador is accredited from [Paris](22989.md). Monaco's ambassador to Australia is accredited from its Ministry of Foreign Affairs. It has an honorary consulate-general in Melbourne. Princess Charlene, Princess Consort of Monaco, visited Australia in 2012 to inaugurate an exhibition on Princess Grace in Bendigo, Victoria. |
| [Montenegro](20760.md) | 1 September 2006 | See Australia–Montenegro relations Australia recognised the Republic of Montenegro on 27 June 2006. Australia is represented in Montenegro through a non-resident ambassador based in the Australian embassy in Belgrade (Serbia). [Australia Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade about relations with Montenegro](http://www.dfat.gov.au/geo/montenegro/index.html) |
| [Netherlands](21148.md) | 18 March 1942 | See [Australia–Netherlands relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Netherlands_relations) Australia has an embassy in [The Hague](30269.md) The Netherlands has an embassy in Canberra. |
| [North Macedonia](23564616.md) | 20 October 1995 | See [Australia–North Macedonia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93North_Macedonia_relations) Both countries established diplomatic relations on 15 February 1994.[^cite_note-675] Australia is represented in North Macedonia by its embassy in [Belgrade](55904.md), Serbia and an honorary consulate in [Skopje](29618.md).[^cite_note-676] North Macedonia has an embassy in [Canberra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canberra).[^cite_note-677] |
| [Norway](21241.md) | 23 June 1947 | See [Australia–Norway relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Norway_relations) Australia has an honorary consulate in [Oslo](22309.md), and is represented in Norway through its embassy in [Copenhagen](5166.md) ([Denmark](76972.md)). Norway has an embassy in Canberra. |
| [Poland](22936.md) | 20 February 1972[^cite_note-Poland-678] | See [Australia–Poland relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Poland_relations) The Polish Dozynki Festival in Adelaide, Australia. Australia has a large community of residents of Polish birth or descent who maintain cultural links with Poland. Australia has an embassy in [Warsaw](32908.md).[^cite_note-679] Poland has an embassy in Canberra and a consulate-general in Sydney.[^cite_note-680] It also has an honorary consulate-general in Melbourne and honorary consulates in Adelaide, Brisbane, Hobart and Perth.[^cite_note-681] Poland established consulates in Sydney in 1919 and Melbourne in 1931. During the Second World War, the two countries were wartime allies. Australian and Polish forces fought alongside each other in the Siege of Tobruk in 1941, and a number of Australian aircrews flew in support of the Warsaw Uprising in 1944. In 1943–1944, during the rupture of relations between the [Polish government-in-exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_government-in-exile) and the [Union of Soviet Socialist Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Socialist_Republics), Australia served as the protecting power for Poland in the Soviet Union, the first time it had played this diplomatic role.[^cite_note-682] Following the establishment of a post-war Communist government in Poland, official relations were more limited. Diplomatic relations were formally established in February 1972. After a brief period of non-resident accreditation from the embassy in [Moscow](19004.md), an Australian resident embassy was opened in Warsaw in 1973. Over 45,000 Australian residents are Polish-born and 184,000 people of Polish ancestry live in Australia. The Polish community is active in promoting people-to-people contact and commercial, cultural and academic ties through various community organisations, bilateral business councils and institutes. In 2014 Poland and Australia signed a Memorandum on a bilateral Work and Holiday Maker Visa Arrangement. Australia's two-way goods and services trade with Poland was valued at nearly A\$2.0 billion in 2019, dominated by merchandise imports from Poland. Australian investment in Poland is valued at around A\$1.2 billion.[^cite_note-683] In August 2018 President [Andrzej Duda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrzej_Duda) made the first ever official visit of a Polish president to Australia.[^cite_note-Poland-678] President [Aleksander Kwaśniewski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksander_Kwa%C5%9Bniewski) had earlier paid a visit to Australia to attend the opening ceremony of the 2000 Summer Olympics being held in Sydney. Australian governor-general [David Hurley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Hurley) paid an official visit to Poland in August 2022 to celebrate the 50th anniversary of diplomatic relations.[^cite_note-684] |
| [Portugal](23033.md) | 15 August 1960 | Formal diplomatic relations between Australia and Portugal were announced on 15 August 1960.[^cite_note-685] Australia has an embassy in Lisbon. For a detailed history of Australian representation in Portugal see [List of ambassadors of Australia to Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_Australia_to_Portugal). Portugal has an embassy in Canberra and a consulate-general in Sydney. It also has honorary consulates in Adelaide, Brisbane, Darwin, Melbourne and Perth. With the end of World War II and the return of Portuguese authority to its territory in Timor, the Australian Government established a consulate in Dili, commencing operations on 1 January 1946.[^cite_note-nla_1946-375] Following the establishment of an Australian Embassy in Lisbon in 1970, the consulate was closed in 1971. The two countries disagreed on East Timor during the period of Indonesian occupation and incorporation of the former Portuguese territory but today work closely in ensuring stability there and giving foreign aid. According to the 2016 census, 61,886 Australians claim Portuguese ancestry while 15,804 are Portuguese-born. Australia and Portugal signed a work and holiday visa arrangement in September 2014.[^cite_note-686] The global economic crisis and the associated downturn in the Portuguese economy had a dramatic impact on Australian merchandise exports to Portugal, which more than halved to around A\$16.6 million by 2016 but have since risen to A\$59 million in 2020. On the other hand, Portuguese exports to Australia have grown in recent years, with merchandise exports totaling A\$318 million in 2020.[^cite_note-687] President [Aníbal Cavaco Silva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An%C3%ADbal_Cavaco_Silva) visited Australia in 2012. |
| [Romania](25445.md) | 18 March 1968 | See [Australia–Romania relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Romania_relations) Australia has a non-resident ambassador in its embassy in [Athens](1216.md) ([Greece](12108.md)), and has a consulate general in [Bucharest](36877.md). Romania has an embassy in Canberra and a general consulate in Melbourne and Sydney.[^cite_note-688] Romania and Australia have concluded an Investment Promotion and Protection Agreement, signed in 1994, a Trade and Economic Agreement (signed with full effect for Australia in July 2002 and for Romania in January 2003) and an Agreement for the Avoidance of Double Taxation and the Prevention of Fiscal Evasion, signed in 2001.[^cite_note-689] |
| [Russia](25391.md) | 10 October 1942,(broken from 23 April 1954 to 16 March 1959) | See [Australia–Russia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Russia_relations) The first Australian embassy in Moscow opened in 1943. Australia has an [embassy in Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Australia_in_Moscow) and two honorary consulates (in [Saint Petersburg](24320051.md) and [Vladivostok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladivostok)).[^cite_note-690] Russia has an embassy in Canberra and a consulate-general in Sydney.[^cite_note-691] [Australian Department of Foreign Affaires and Trade about the relation with Russia](http://www.dfat.gov.au/geo/russia/index.html) |
| [San Marino](27248.md) | 13 September 1995[^cite_note-Rapporti-210] | Australia and San Marino formalized diplomatic relations with the non-resident accreditation of an Australian ambassador, based in Rome. San Marino has honorary consulates in Sydney and Perth. A reciprocal Working Holiday Maker visa program between Australia and San Marino took effect on 1 January 2017. |
| [Serbia](29265.md) | 26 April 1966 | See [Australia–Serbia relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Serbia_relations) Australia has an embassy in [Belgrade](55904.md). Serbia has an embassy in Canberra and a consulate-general in Sydney. The European office of the [Australian Federal Police](341221.md) is located in Belgrade as of 2003. In the 2006 Australian Census, 95,364 people identified themselves as having Serbian origin. |
| [Slovakia](26830.md) | 1 January 1993 | Australia's embassy in [Vienna](55866.md) is accredited to Slovakia. Slovakia maintained an embassy in Canberra for many years but this was closed in 2024. Representation is maintained through a consulate-general in Sydney and honorary consulates in Brisbane, Melbourne and Perth. Two-way trade between the countries stands at \$115 million. There are approximately 10,000 people of Slovak origin living in Australia.[^cite_note-692] |
| [Slovenia](27338.md) | 5 February 1992 | Australia recognised Slovenia as an independent state on 16 January 1992.[^cite_note-693] Australia is represented in Slovenian through its embassy in [Vienna](55866.md), Austria, and through an honorary consulate in [Ljubljana](42374.md). Slovenia has an embassy in Canberra and an honorary consulate in [Camperdown, New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camperdown,_New_South_Wales).[^cite_note-694] There are between 20,000 and 25,000 Slovenians who live in Australia. [Australian Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade about relations with Slovenia](http://www.dfat.gov.au/geo/slovenia/index.html) |
| [Spain](26667.md) | 26 October 1967 | See [Australia–Spain relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Spain_relations) Australia has an embassy in [Madrid](41188263.md). Spain has an embassy in Canberra and consulates-general in Melbourne and Sydney. |
| [Sweden](5058739.md) | 26 September 1947[^cite_note-695] | See [Australia–Sweden relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Sweden_relations) Australia and Sweden have a long-standing friendly relationship with migration and trading links extending back to the nineteenth century. In 1851 the first Swedish-Norwegian Consuls were appointed in Sydney, Melbourne and Port Adelaide. The first Swedish Consul-General was appointed in 1906.[^cite_note-696] Formal diplomatic relations were established in 1947 with the opening of a Swedish legation in Sydney, which moved in 1951 to Canberra. An Australian legation was established in Stockholm in 1961, and diplomatic relations were raised to ambassadorial level in 1963.[^cite_note-nla_Australian_Representation_Overseas-697] For a detailed history of Australian diplomatic representation in Sweden see [List of ambassadors of Australia to Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ambassadors_of_Australia_to_Sweden). Australia has an embassy in [Stockholm](26741.md). Sweden has an embassy in Canberra, an honorary consulate-general in Sydney and honorary consulates in Adelaide, Brisbane, Cairns, Darwin, Hobart, Melbourne and Perth. The two governments cooperate closely in multilateral organizations. King Carl XVI Gustaf made state visits to Australia in 1982 and 2005. There were 46,669 Australian residents of Swedish descent and 10,847 born in Sweden according to the 2021 Australian census. There has been a working holiday visa agreement in place since 2001. Australia and Sweden are important trading partners. In 2021, two-way goods and services trade was valued at A\$3.4 billion, the stock of Australian investment in Sweden was valued at A\$15.0 billion and Swedish investment in Australia was valued at A\$10.2 billion. |
| [Switzerland](26748.md) | 2 June 1961 | See [Australia–Switzerland relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Switzerland_relations) Switzerland opened a consulate in Sydney in 1855 and one in Melbourne in 1856. Australia has an embassy in [Bern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bern) and a consulate-general in [Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva). Switzerland has an embassy in Canberra, a consulate-general in Sydney and six honorary consulates in Adelaide, Brisbane, Darwin, Hobart, Melbourne and Perth. [Australian Department of Foreign Affairs and Trade about relations with Switzerland](http://www.dfat.gov.au/geo/switzerland/switzerland_brief.html) [Swiss Federal Department of Foreign Affairs about relations with Australia](http://www.eda.admin.ch/eda/en/home/reps/ocea/vaus/bilaus.html) |
| [Ukraine](31750.md) | 10 January 1992[^cite_note-Asia_and_Oceania_countries-194] | See [Australia–Ukraine relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93Ukraine_relations) The Sydney Opera House is lit in the Ukrainian national colours in solidarity with Ukraine in the face of Russian aggression, 4 March 2022. Australia recognised Ukraine's independence on 26 December 1991[^cite_note-Australia_recognizes_the_11_republics_but_not_Georgia-434] upon the dissolution of the [Union of Soviet Socialist Republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Soviet_Socialist_Republics) and established diplomatic relations early the following year. Ukraine opened an embassy in Canberra in March 2003.[^cite_note-698] Australia opened an embassy in Kyiv in November 2014. Following the Russian attack in February 2022, it has been temporarily relocated to Poland. The 2016 Census recorded 13,366 Ukrainian-born persons in Australia. Most Ukrainian migrants to Australia arrived in the post-World War II period. The small but active Ukrainian community in Australia plays an important role in developing bilateral relations. In 2002 the Australian Federation of Ukrainian Organisations helped establish Ukrainian-Australian House in Kyiv to promote commercial ties. [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko) made the first visit to Australia by a Ukrainian president in 2014. On 1 April 2016, Australia and Ukraine signed a Nuclear Cooperation Agreement, enabling Australia to export uranium to Ukraine – one of the world's top ten generators of nuclear power. This agreement entered into force in June 2017. The Agreement allows Australia to help Ukraine diversify its energy supply, and enhances bilateral cooperation between Australia and Ukraine on nuclear-related activities, including nuclear safeguards, security, safety and science. Prior to the 2022 Russian attack on Ukraine, bilateral trade had been modest – in 2021–2022 two-way goods and services trade was valued at A\$243 million.[^cite_note-699] Australia has been a vigorous supporter of Ukraine's sovereignty and territorial integrity since the start of the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md) in 2014 and particularly since its escalation in February 2022 – see [Russian invasion of Ukraine (2022–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)). Australia is contributing important defence, economic and humanitarian assistance to Ukraine, and has imposed sanctions on Russian and Belarusian individuals and organisations connected with Russian aggression. President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) addressed the Australian Parliament by video on 31 March 2022.[^cite_note-700] Prime Minister [Anthony Albanese](556745.md) visited Kyiv on 3 July 2022, to meet with President Zelenskyy and show support to Ukraine.[^cite_note-701] |
| [United Kingdom](31717.md) |  | See [Australia–United Kingdom relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_relations) Australia House, which houses the [High Commission of Australia, London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Commission_of_Australia,_London) was opened on August 3, 1918. British-Australian relations are close, marked by shared history, culture, institutions and language, extensive people-to-people links, aligned security interests, and substantial trade and investment cooperation. Australia has a high commission in [London](17867.md), established in 1909. It is Australia's oldest diplomatic mission. For a detailed history of Australia's representation in the United Kingdom see [List of high commissioners of Australia to the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_commissioners_of_Australia_to_the_United_Kingdom). The United Kingdom has a high commission in Canberra, established in 1936, making it the oldest diplomatic mission in the capital, and consulates-general in [Brisbane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brisbane), [Melbourne](17306237.md), [Perth](24355.md) and [Sydney](27862.md) and honorary consulates in [Adelaide](1148.md), [Alice Springs](183935.md), [Cairns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairns) and [Hobart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hobart). For a detailed history of British representation in Australia see [List of high commissioners of the United Kingdom to Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_commissioners_of_the_United_Kingdom_to_Australia). Modern-day Australia was established in 1901 from a federation of the six British colonies in Australia. Upon federation, Australia became a Dominion of the [British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire). Formal adoption by Australia in 1942 of the [Statute of Westminster 1931](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statute_of_Westminster_1931) confirmed its full autonomy from the United Kingdom, including in the conduct of its foreign relations. The promulgation of the [Australia Act 1986](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_Act_1986) by both Australian and British Parliaments effectively eliminated any residual constitutional links between the two countries, though they continue to share the same monarch. [World War II](32927.md) and Australia's subsequent [ANZUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ANZUS) military alliance with the [United States](3434750.md), the evolution of the British Empire into the [Commonwealth of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations), and the trade reorientation of the United Kingdom away from the Commonwealth towards Europe upon joining the [European Economic Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Economic_Community) in 1973 gradually transformed the Australia-UK bilateral relationship over the second half of the 20th century. British-born people have been the largest overseas-born group in Australia since British colonization began in 1788 and the United Kingdom remains a top three source country of migrants to Australia. The 2016 Australian census identified 1,087,759 United Kingdom-born people in Australia.[^cite_note-702] There is significant visitor exchange, with 718,600 UK visitors to Australia and 667 800 Australian visitors in the UK as of June 2019.[^cite_note-703] Right of British citizenship or abode for descendants of British migrants, and a reciprocal working holiday scheme, makes the United Kingdom a popular destination for Australians living overseas. Australia excels in many sports that originate in the UK, and the two countries enjoy a close sporting rivalry. Annual Australia-UK Ministerial Consultations (AUKMIN) are the premier bilateral fora on foreign policy, defence and security issues. The UK is the second largest source of foreign investment in Australia, with the stock of investment valued at A\$574.8 billion in 2018. Australian investment in the UK was A\$408 billion in 2018. The UK is Australia's 8th largest two-way trading partner, worth A\$26.9 billion in 2018, and its third largest services trading partner, with Australian services exports to the UK of A\$5.5 billion and imports of A\$9.2 billion in 2018. The [Australia–United Kingdom Free Trade Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia%E2%80%93United_Kingdom_Free_Trade_Agreement), signed in December 2021 was the first trade agreement signed by Britain since leaving the European Union that was negotiated completely anew. Australia supports Britain's accession to the [Comprehensive and Progressive Agreement for Trans-Pacific Partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_and_Progressive_Agreement_for_Trans-Pacific_Partnership). The Australia-UK Defence and Security Cooperation Treaty signed in 2013 consolidates various elements of long-standing military/defence cooperation. Australia and the UK are signatories of the [Five Power Defence Arrangements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Power_Defence_Arrangements) (FPDA), a series of bilateral defence relationships established by multi-lateral agreements between Australia, Malaysia, New Zealand, Singapore, and the United Kingdom. Both countries are members of the [Five Eyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Five_Eyes) intelligence-sharing alliance. The [AUKUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AUKUS) trilateral security partnership announced in 2021 deepens the Australia-UK defence partnership. In 2006, Prime Minister [Tony Blair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Blair) became the first British head of government to address the Australian Parliament. |

### Oceania

See also: [Arc of Instability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arc_of_Instability), [Pacific Solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Solution), and [RAMSI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAMSI)

Australia is a member of the [Pacific Islands Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islands_Forum) and other regional organisations. As part of its Pacific Step-Up initiative announced in 2016[^cite_note-Stepping-704] Australia has uniquely established resident High Commissions and embassies in all independent and self governing members of the Pacific Islands Forum as well as consulates-general in New Caledonia and French Polynesia. Australia provides aid to many of its developing Pacific Islands neighbours, and to Papua New Guinea. For decades, it has been the largest donor of aid to the Oceania region.[^cite_note-705] China and New Zealand, the next biggest donors, donated only one sixth of Australia's aid during the 2010s.[^cite_note-706]

Since the end of the Cold War, the understanding from the United States has been that Australia and New Zealand would assume responsibility for the security of much of the Oceania region, whom they already share pre-existing cultural and economic ties to.[^cite_note-707]

Australia's approach to the Pacific has included frequent references to what it has perceived as an "[Arc of Instability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arc_of_Instability)" among its island neighbours. In August 2006 Australian defence minister [Brendan Nelson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brendan_Nelson) stated to the Australian Parliament:

> We cannot afford to have failing states in our region. The so-called 'arc of instability', which basically goes from East Timor through to the south-west Pacific states, means that not only does Australia have a responsibility in preventing and indeed assisting with humanitarian and disaster relief, but also that we cannot allow any of these countries to become havens for transnational crime, nor indeed havens for terrorism.[^cite_note-708]

As from early 2008, the Australian government led by [Kevin Rudd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Rudd) began what it called a "new approach" to relations between Australia and the Pacific, appointing a Parliamentary Secretary for Pacific Island Affairs, Duncan Kerr. In February, Kerr and fellow Parliamentary Secretary for Foreign Affairs, Bob McMullan visited Samoa, Tonga and Kiribati in February, and stated:

> Broadly, the approach is one of much more partnership and engagement on the basis of mutual respect. We're not going to be lecturing or hectoring, we're going to try and work together with them and I think we set a pretty good standard with the way we started. The relationships we've established with ministers and leaders in those countries \[Kiribati, Tonga and Samoa] is very positive.[^cite_note-709]

[Richard Marles](4386805.md), the deputy leader of the Australian Labor Party, has strongly advocated for Australia to prioritize its role in the Pacific. In 2021, he wrote a book titled *Tides that bind: Australia in the Pacific*, and claimed in an interview that, "By any measure, we are huge part of the Pacific. We're the largest donor into the Pacific, we've got the biggest diplomatic footprint in the Pacific, we've got the most development resources in the Pacific of any country. For most of the Pacific, we're the most important bilateral relationship they have, more important than the United States, more important than China."[^cite_note-710]

| Country | Formal relations began | Notes |
